${\mathcal{S}}\overset{d}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{H}}\overset{}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathcal{S}}'$. Thus ${\mathcal{H}}$ and ${\mathcal{H}}'$ are Hilbert--Gelfand spaces (Items
1 and 2), mutually dual under the extended pairing.

Finally, for $\varphi,\psi\in{\mathcal{S}}$,
\begin{align*}
\langle{\mathrm{J}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\varphi,\psi\rangle_{{\mathcal{H}}'\times{\mathcal{H}}}
=\langle\varphi,\psi\rangle_{{\mathcal{H}}}
=\langle{\mathrm{A}}\varphi,\psi\rangle,
\end{align*}
so that the realization of ${\mathrm{J}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\varphi$ in ${\mathcal{S}}'$ is ${\mathrm{A}}\varphi$; by density,
${\mathrm{J}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}$ is the unique continuous extension of ${\mathrm{A}}$, and ${\mathrm{J}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}^{-1}$ that of
${\mathrm{A}}^{-1}$. This proves the converse part. If, moreover, ${\mathcal{H}}$ is prescribed as in Item 4,
then its norm agrees with $|\cdot|_{{\mathrm{A}}}$ on the dense subspace ${\mathcal{S}}$, so that the identity on
${\mathcal{S}}$ extends to an isometric isomorphism onto the realization above, through which ${\mathcal{H}}$
inherits the embeddings; this proves $4\Rightarrow1$.

\medskip
\emph{Step 3 ($2\Rightarrow1$)}:
Let us assume that ${\mathcal{H}}'$ is a Hilbert--Gelfand space. Step 1, applied to ${\mathcal{H}}'$,
provides a compatible Riesz pair $({\mathrm{A}}_1,{\mathrm{A}}_1^{-1})$ with
$|\varphi|_{{\mathrm{A}}_1}=\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{H}}'}$ and
$|\varphi|_{{\mathrm{A}}_1^{-1}}=\|\varphi\|_{({\mathcal{H}}')'}=\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{H}}}$, where the last
equality uses the reflexivity of ${\mathcal{H}}$. The swapped pair $({\mathrm{A}}_1^{-1},{\mathrm{A}}_1)$ is again
a compatible Riesz pair, so that Step 2 realizes
$\overline{({\mathcal{S}},\|\cdot\|_{{\mathcal{H}}})}={\mathcal{H}}$ as a Hilbert--Gelfand space, which is Item 1.

\medskip
\emph{Step 4 ($1\Leftrightarrow3$)}:
The implication $1\Rightarrow3$ is immediate. Conversely, the inclusion $\jmath$ has a closed
graph: if $\varphi_n\to\varphi$ in ${\mathcal{S}}$ and $\jmath\varphi_n\to u$ in ${\mathcal{H}}$, then
$\iota\varphi_n=\kappa\jmath\varphi_n$ converges to both $\iota\varphi$ and $\kappa u$, which
implies that $u=\jmath\varphi$ by the injectivity of $\kappa$. Since ${\mathcal{S}}$ is barrelled and
${\mathcal{H}}$ is a Banach space, the closed-graph theorem (in Pt\'ak's form, for barrelled domains
and $B$-complete ranges) \cite{Ptak1958} (see also \cite[Ch.~IV]{Schaefer-Wolff1999}) yields the
continuity of $\jmath$; its range is dense by the
standing hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{Theo:HilbertPositiveDef}. The range of $\kappa$ contains
$\iota({\mathcal{S}})$ and is dense in ${\mathcal{S}}'$. Hence Item 1 holds.

\medskip
\emph{Step 5 (Compatibility of the pairings)}:
For $f\in{\mathcal{H}}'$, realized in ${\mathcal{S}}'$ as $\jmath^\ast f$, and for $\varphi\in{\mathcal{S}}$, the
definition of the adjoint gives
\begin{align*}
\langle\jmath^\ast f,\varphi\rangle
=\langle f,\jmath\varphi\rangle_{{\mathcal{H}}'\times{\mathcal{H}}},
\end{align*}
which is \eqref{Eq:DualityProdH}. The extension to $(f,g)\in{\mathcal{H}}'\times{\mathcal{H}}$ and the bound
\eqref{Eq:HDualityBound} follow from the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for the dual pairing of
${\mathcal{H}}$.

\medskip
\emph{Step 6 (Uniqueness of the pair)}:
Let $({\mathrm{A}}_2,{\mathrm{A}}_2^{-1})$ be another compatible Riesz pair with
$|\cdot|_{{\mathrm{A}}_2}=\|\cdot\|_{{\mathcal{H}}}$ on ${\mathcal{S}}$. By Step 2, it generates a
Hilbert--Gelfand space whose norm agrees with that of ${\mathcal{H}}$ on the dense subspace
${\mathcal{S}}$; the identity map on ${\mathcal{S}}$ extends to an isometric isomorphism between the two
spaces and, since both embeddings into ${\mathcal{S}}'$ are continuous and the weak-$\ast$ topology
is Hausdorff, the two spaces coincide as subsets of ${\mathcal{S}}'$ with equal norms. In particular,
they share the same Riesz map, and Step 2 identifies the realization of
${\mathrm{J}}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\varphi$ in ${\mathcal{S}}'$ both as ${\mathrm{A}}\varphi$ and as
${\mathrm{A}}_2\varphi$; hence ${\mathrm{A}}_2={\mathrm{A}}$, the symmetry of both operators being automatic by
Remark~\ref{Rem:AutomaticSymmetry}. The same argument, applied to
${\mathcal{H}}'$, gives $({\mathrm{A}}_2)^{-1}={\mathrm{A}}^{-1}$. This completes the proof.
